The Ghost Rockets
Between the "foo fighters" of World War II, and the first American UFO wave of
ightings of flying discs and flying saucers in 1947, there was a major Scandinavian wav
in 1946. of sightings of UFO-like obiects called the Swedish Ghost Rockets
3
While many of them were shaped like long-range rockets of the German V-2
style, they mostly flew level, and not too fast, and silent. None of these characteristics
came close to describing any type of rocket, then or later. This placed them squarely in
the proto-UFO category. Nevertheless, there was a great effort to blame the sightings on
Soviet tests of captured V-2s from across the Baltic Sea from Sweden. No such tests ever
took place.
While the Ghost Rockets did not resemble the discs and saucers of the 1947 wave,
they fit into the broad UFO category because they were Unidentified, they were Flying,
and they gave every indication of being Objects. An estimated 1,500 reports were made
to the Swedish Defence Staff, making this a major event in UFO history.
Catalogs of "Ghost Rocket" sightings have been published, and descriptions of
the wave have appeared in dozens of UFO histories. But for the first time, we present a
large collection of official government documents relating to the "Ghost Rockets" and
indicative of the great interest at high levels of several governments.
Supporting this material are about 100 clippings from newspapers in many
different countries, to show the public interest in strange things flying through the sky.
Credit for acquiring and assembling this material goes to Jan Aldrich, an active
member of NICAP in the 1960's. In the 1990's, after a career in the U.S. Army, he
returned to the fray and resumed his position as one of the top researchers. His initial
project was the 1947 wave, for which he criss-crossed the USA to study hundreds of
newspapers not covered in Ted Bloecher's "The UFO Wave of 1947", published by
NICAP. Aldrich's work has expanded the number of known sightings in the summer of
1947 from 700+ to more than 1,500. And added large chunks of the globe to the scope of
the wave.
He has brought this same intensity to his study of the Ghost Rockets, and to his
work as the leader of Project 47, a small group of historians concentrating on the early
days of UFOs. Little by little, the inside story of UFOs prior to 1947 is being revealed.
You are invited to contact Aldrich directly with additional information on the
Ghost Rockets: www.iuforg.org or jan@CYBERZONE.NET

"GHOST ROCKETS" OVER
SCANDINAVIA
flying missiles were first reported over southern Sweden in
jale May 1946 by the press, which gave the missiles the namc
of "Chost Rockets." In Junc, the missiles also nad been
reported over linland and Denmark. By July, the number ol
sightings over Sweden had greatly increased; and seyeral also
had been reportçd over Norway. The great majority of these
reports were made by untrained observers and, as would be
expected, varied widely in the description of the actual
missiles as well as, of their course, altitude, and speed.
Descriptions of the Missile
The two most common descriptions of the missiles were
"it hell of fire with a tail" and a "shiny cigur-shaped object."
The reported direction of flight covored all points of the
compass, with i northerly
direction being slightly, pre-
dominane. Variations in altitude ranged from trectop heiglit
do 160,000 feet, the higher altitudes almost exclusively being
reported from Finland, Speeds reported were from 65 me p. h.
1o "lightning fost," with the majority described as having
art or very great speed. The missiles generally have been
described in horizontal flight; a few have been reported as
diving into the ground or into lakes, or exploding in the air.
In no case have fragments been found other than bits of
material described as "nonnetallic slag."
One of the few reports available from an individual who
should be more reliablo and competont than the average:

SWEDEN
Experimental Jet Fighter
Makes First Flight
Sweden's esperinestal jet fighter, the 1.29.
completed its first light
September at
Laskafing.
The aircraft reportedly remained
aloft two and a half bours.
The J.29 has a swept-back wing, is powered
by & De Havilland "Ghost" turbojet engine
rated at 5,000 pounds thrust for takeoff. The
craft is designed for a maximum speed of 568
knots at sen level, and u service ceiling of
45,000 feet. (Secret]
First Mosquitoes Arrive;
60 Due by July 1949
De Havilland Mosquitoes, the first of 60 to
be supplied by July 1949, have arrived in
Sweden. Rate of delivery is to be stepped up to
6 a month.
The aircraft are delivered
complete night fighter equipment, including
radar, and will be placed in three night fighter
squadrons.
The taree squadrons will improve
the defensive capabilities of the Swedish Air
Air Force.
[Secret]
Fires in the Sky
An increase in the number of incidents over
Sweden during the summer may be connected
with Soviet guided missile tests in the Lake
Seliger area (57° N.-33° E.) or along the Baltic
coast of Estonia.
About 32 launching sites reporterly exist in
areas fronting on the Baltic Sez.
Swedisha
sources claim to have confirmed the existence of
three such sites.

Alarmed
Under & Stockholm dateline, the New York
Times recently reported that, "Alarmed by the
present international tension, the Swedes have
taken a number of military security measures.
Some of these measures are understood to go
beyond any
preparations the Swedes made
during World War II.
While their nature is
secret, it is known that sea and land minefields
have been laid at vulnerable spots."
Reserve officers are rushed through training
periods and lome guard dril is pushed effer-
tively. Air Force training has been intensified.
Measures have been prepared to deal with the
fifta column. The New York Times also stated

considerably. The country can put 600,000 men
